# PDU Manager Live-Service Capture and GitHub Commit Plan

**Date:** 2026-09-26  
**Target repository:** `https://github.com/startupteams/pdu-marion-ia-usa-project-framework`  
**Production host:** Proxmox node `MIAM-00133`  
**Production guest:** VM `154`  
**Current web UI:** `https://10.0.20.154/`  
**Execution mode:** Autonomous discovery and capture, with explicit stop gates for secrets, destructive actions, significant architecture changes, and merge/production deployment.

## 1. Mission

Capture the complete, currently running PDU Manager service from VM154 into the GitHub repository so that the repository becomes an understandable, reviewable, reproducible source of truth.

The agent must capture enough information to answer all of the following from GitHub alone:

1. What the PDU Manager is for and why the business operates it.
2. What code actually serves `https://10.0.20.154/`.
3. How the service is started and kept running.
4. What runtime, packages, services, files, configuration, certificates, and external integrations it depends on.
5. Which configuration is safe to commit and which values are secrets or site-local state.
6. How a clean VM can be made to host the same service.
7. How to validate the service without turning any PDU outlet on, off, or rebooting it.
8. Which behavior is implemented today and which behavior is only planned.

The final code capture must be committed on a branch and presented for human review. **Do not merge the branch autonomously.**

## 2. Business reason

The PDU Manager is operational infrastructure for the MARION-IA-USA server environment. It centralizes remote visibility and controlled power operations for rack equipment connected to network-managed PDUs. Capturing the service in GitHub is required because a service that exists only as mutable files on a production VM is difficult to reproduce, review, test, recover, or safely modify.

Moving the source into GitHub provides:

- disaster-recovery capability if VM154 is lost;
- traceable changes to power-control logic and outlet metadata;
- human review before production changes;
- reproducible deployment instead of one-off shell edits;
- automated validation and secret scanning;
- a clear handoff point for AI agents and humans;
- a safe foundation for CI/CD;
- protection against accidental configuration drift between documentation, source, and the running service.

## 3. Known production context to preserve and verify

Treat these as **known environment facts / intended behavior that must be verified against the live VM**, not as permission to invent implementation details.

- The PDU Manager runs on VM154 hosted by Proxmox node `MIAM-00133`.
- The user reaches the service at `https://10.0.20.154/`.
- The environment contains three network-managed PDU endpoints at `10.0.20.151`, `10.0.20.152`, and `10.0.20.153`.
- The UI currently identifies the backend as a direct SSH / PowerAlert style control path; the exact implementation must be verified from code and runtime configuration.
- LLDAP at `10.0.20.101` has been used as the authentication directory for this environment; verify whether and how VM154 currently uses it.
- Current UI capabilities to preserve include outlet state display, outlet naming, individual ON/OFF/REBOOT actions, batch selection/actions, presets, protection/lockout behavior, recent-action logging, configuration save/upload behavior, and a mobile-usable web UI. Verify each capability against the live service before documenting it as implemented.
- Critical devices must be protected from casual shutdown/reboot. In particular, historical design intent protects `MIAM-00133` from dangerous power actions. Verify the current protection model from live configuration/code.
- KVM-related naming has recently been updated. The expected current naming includes:
  - `MIAM-00172 - JetKVM Hardware Console`
  - `MIAM-00182 - TESmart 16-Port HDMI KVM`
  Verify the live configuration and do not reintroduce deprecated KVM labels.

## 4. Non-negotiable safety rules

The capture task is **read-only against production behavior** except for creating temporary capture files and, later, making explicitly reviewed source-control changes in a separate workspace.

The agent MUST NOT during capture or validation:

- send PDU `OFF`, `ON`, `REBOOT`, `CYCLE`, or equivalent actuation commands;
- click production power-control buttons as a test;
- reboot or shut down VM154 or `MIAM-00133`;
- change production outlet names, presets, lockouts, protection flags, credentials, authentication settings, firewall rules, or network configuration;
- rotate secrets;
- edit the running service in place merely to make it easier to capture;
- overwrite production configuration with repository files;
- commit credentials, passwords, tokens, private keys, session secrets, TLS private keys, LLDAP bind credentials, or PDU credentials;
- merge its own pull request.

If any action is required that could alter power state, infrastructure, permissions, secrets, or production data, STOP and request human approval.

## 5. Repository rules

Before making repository changes, read:

1. `README.md`
2. `AGENTS.md`
3. `REQUIREMENTS.md`
4. `SPRINT.md`
5. `FUTURE_WORK.md`
6. `docs/ARCHITECTURE.md`
7. `docs/adr/`
8. `docs/tdr/`

Follow the repository's human-approval model.

If no issue/ticket already exists for this work, create one before committing so branch and commit naming can comply with repository policy.

Suggested issue title:

`Capture live PDU Manager source and establish reproducible deployment baseline`

Suggested branch after the issue number is known:

`chore/<ISSUE>-capture-live-pdu-manager`

Suggested commit subject after the issue number is known:

`chore(capture): capture live PDU manager baseline (#<ISSUE>)`

Do not commit directly to `main`.

## 6. Phase A - Establish a production inventory without changing production

From the `MIAM-00133` root shell, begin by proving VM154 exists and is running:

```bash
qm status 154
qm config 154
```

Use the QEMU guest agent where available:

```bash
qm guest exec 154 -- bash -lc 'hostname; date; uname -a; cat /etc/os-release'
```

Capture the output into the work log.

### 6.1 Discover the serving process

Inside VM154, gather:

```bash
systemctl list-units --type=service --all --no-pager
systemctl list-unit-files --type=service --no-pager
ps auxww
ss -lntup
```

Identify the process listening on TCP 443 and any internal application port. For each candidate process, capture:

- PID;
- command line;
- executable path;
- working directory;
- user/group;
- parent process;
- systemd unit or container name;
- environment-file references;
- restart policy;
- log destination.

Useful read-only commands include:

```bash
readlink -f /proc/<PID>/exe
readlink -f /proc/<PID>/cwd
tr '\0' ' ' < /proc/<PID>/cmdline
systemctl status <unit> --no-pager
systemctl cat <unit>
systemctl show <unit>
```

Do not copy `/proc/<PID>/environ` into the repository. It may contain secrets.

### 6.2 Determine the runtime model

Explicitly determine whether the live service uses any of the following:

- Python / Flask / FastAPI / Gunicorn / Uvicorn;
- Node.js;
- Docker / Docker Compose;
- nginx / Apache / Caddy / another reverse proxy;
- a standalone HTTPS development server;
- SQLite / JSON / YAML / flat-file configuration;
- systemd only;
- cron or systemd timers;
- custom shell wrappers.

Record facts only. Do not migrate to a different runtime during capture.

### 6.3 Find live files, not backups

The earlier KVM-label search revealed many historical copies under `/root`, including backup directories and bootstrap scripts. That is evidence that broad string searches are not sufficient to identify the live application.

The agent must derive the live code path from the running process/service/container first. Then inspect only that live path and its declared dependencies.

For every discovered file/directory, classify it as one of:

- **SOURCE** - application code that belongs in Git;
- **DEPLOYMENT** - service units, reverse-proxy config, installer/bootstrap logic;
- **SAFE CONFIG** - non-secret defaults or site mapping appropriate for Git;
- **SECRET CONFIG** - credentials/keys/tokens; never commit;
- **PERSISTENT STATE** - logs, runtime state, mutable uploaded configuration, databases;
- **GENERATED** - caches, bytecode, build output;
- **BACKUP/LEGACY** - historical copies not used by the live service;
- **UNKNOWN** - requires human review.

### 6.4 Capture service/dependency metadata

Capture, when applicable:

- systemd unit files and drop-ins;
- reverse-proxy configuration;
- application source;
- static assets/templates;
- dependency manifests (`requirements.txt`, `pyproject.toml`, `package.json`, lockfiles, etc.);
- exact runtime versions;
- package/service prerequisites;
- file ownership and permissions needed to run;
- listener ports;
- application health behavior;
- log locations;
- persistent data/config locations;
- TLS termination location;
- authentication integration;
- PDU integration points;
- startup/restart commands.

If dependency manifests do not exist, use the live environment as evidence to create a minimal reproducible manifest, but label it as reconstructed and validate it later on a clean staging VM.

## 7. Phase B - Create a clean capture workspace

Create a workspace **outside the production application directory**. Prefer an agent workstation or a temporary directory on `MIAM-00133`, for example:

```text
/root/pdu-manager-capture-<timestamp>/
```

Do not edit live files.

Transfer only the classified SOURCE, DEPLOYMENT, and SAFE CONFIG files into this workspace.

If SSH/SCP to VM154 is available, use a read-only copy path. If not, use a temporary tar archive inside VM154 and transfer it through an available safe mechanism. Avoid printing binary archives or secrets into terminal history when a direct file-copy mechanism is available.

Generate a capture manifest containing:

- source path on VM154;
- destination path in the repository;
- SHA-256 checksum;
- classification;
- whether the file was changed/sanitized before commit;
- reason for exclusion if not committed.

Save the manifest as `docs/CAPTURE_MANIFEST.md` in the branch.

## 8. Phase C - Secret and sensitive-data review

Before `git add`, inspect all captured files for:

- passwords;
- usernames paired with passwords;
- SSH private keys;
- TLS private keys;
- API tokens;
- cookies/session secrets;
- LDAP bind passwords;
- PDU credentials;
- GitHub credentials;
- embedded Basic Auth URLs;
- `.env` files;
- shell history;
- certificates containing private keys;
- database dumps or logs containing sensitive material.

At minimum run a repository secret scanner such as `gitleaks` if available. Also perform targeted text searches.

For every secret found:

1. Leave the production value unchanged on VM154 during capture.
2. Remove the secret from the Git copy.
3. Replace it with environment/config indirection.
4. Add a safe example such as `.env.example` or `config.example.*` containing placeholder values only.
5. Add the real secret-bearing path to `.gitignore` if needed.
6. Document where production will obtain the secret in `docs/DEPLOYMENT.md` without revealing the value.
7. If a secret was already committed at any point, STOP and request human assistance for rotation/history remediation.

## 9. Phase D - Establish an understandable repository layout

Preserve the application's natural structure when possible. Do not perform a speculative rewrite during capture.

A likely repository shape after discovery may resemble:

```text
/
  README.md
  AGENTS.md
  REQUIREMENTS.md
  FUTURE_WORK.md
  SPRINT.md
  app/ or src/                 # live application source, exact name chosen from discovered stack
  tests/                       # only real tests; do not create fake passing tests
  deploy/
    systemd/                   # captured/reconstructed unit files
    proxy/                     # reverse proxy config if applicable
    scripts/                   # install/deploy/healthcheck scripts
    examples/                  # safe config examples
  docs/
    ARCHITECTURE.md
    OPERATIONS.md
    DEPLOYMENT.md
    CURRENT_STATE_AND_LIMITATIONS.md
    CAPTURE_MANIFEST.md
    adr/
    tdr/
```

Do not force this layout if the live application already has a clear conventional structure. The objective is reproducibility, not cosmetic reorganization.

## 10. Phase E - Document the observed implementation

Update the project documentation package supplied with this plan using facts discovered from VM154.

Every statement about implementation should be marked one of:

- **Implemented and verified** - directly observed in live code/runtime;
- **Implemented but not yet fully validated** - present but not safely exercised;
- **Required target** - approved behavior that the captured code must satisfy;
- **Future work** - explicitly not current scope;
- **Unknown / requires human confirmation** - evidence is insufficient.

Do not claim a power-control action was validated by actuating production hardware.

## 11. Phase F - Reproduce the service without touching production

Before calling the capture complete, prove the repository contains enough information to recreate the application in a clean environment.

Preferred validation order:

1. static dependency/install validation;
2. application start with mock or disabled PDU backend;
3. HTTPS/web UI load;
4. configuration parsing;
5. authentication integration in a non-production mode where practical;
6. read-only status path using mocks or a read-only adapter;
7. no-actuation smoke tests.

Use a disposable staging VM/container rather than VM154 for destructive setup testing.

If a clean deployment cannot be achieved from the repository, do **not** hide the gap. Record every missing dependency/manual step in `docs/CURRENT_STATE_AND_LIMITATIONS.md` and continue improving the captured source until a clean deployment is possible or a human decides to accept temporary technical debt.

## 12. Phase G - Git commit and pull request

Before commit:

```bash
git status
git diff --check
```

Run every project validation command that now exists. Record exact results.

Commit only after:

- live source path is proven from runtime evidence;
- secret scan passes;
- historical backups are excluded;
- generated files/logs are excluded;
- documentation reflects the actual captured stack;
- a clean/staging start has been attempted and results recorded.

Push the feature branch to:

`startupteams/pdu-marion-ia-usa-project-framework`

Open a pull request using the repository template.

The PR must explicitly state:

- live service paths discovered;
- runtime stack discovered;
- systemd/container/proxy units discovered;
- files captured;
- files intentionally excluded;
- secret remediation performed;
- clean deployment test result;
- known limitations;
- requirements covered;
- ADR/TDR changes;
- exact validation performed;
- whether production VM154 was changed (**expected: no functional production change during capture**).

**STOP after PR creation. A human must approve the merge.**

## 13. Completion criteria

The capture task is complete only when all of the following are true:

- [ ] The exact process serving `https://10.0.20.154/` has been identified.
- [ ] The exact live application directory has been identified from runtime evidence.
- [ ] Source code has been copied from the live deployment without relying on old backup folders.
- [ ] Runtime/service/proxy/dependency definitions are in Git or documented reproducibly.
- [ ] Secrets are excluded and externalized.
- [ ] The repository documents business purpose, requirements, architecture, operations, deployment, limitations, and future work.
- [ ] A clean/staging deployment has been attempted from source.
- [ ] Validation results are recorded honestly.
- [ ] No production power actuation occurred during capture/validation.
- [ ] No production infrastructure reboot was performed for capture.
- [ ] The work is committed on a branch and pushed.
- [ ] A pull request exists.
- [ ] The agent has NOT merged its own PR.

## 14. Required agent handoff

At completion, write `docs/handoffs/<date>-source-capture-handoff.md` or provide an equivalent PR handoff containing:

1. issue and branch;
2. commit SHA(s);
3. PR URL;
4. live source path;
5. service unit/process name;
6. runtime and dependency versions;
7. external dependencies;
8. secret/config strategy;
9. staging reproduction result;
10. validation commands/results;
11. risks and limitations;
12. ADR/TDR status;
13. future work discovered;
14. exact next action for the human reviewer.

# PDU Manager Service — Future Work Plan v2

**Date:** 2026-09-27  
**System:** MARION-IA-USA Power Distribution Unit (PDU) Manager  
**Repository:** `startupteams/pdu-marion-ia-usa-project-framework`  
**Current production:** VM154 on MIAM-00133  
**Target production:** VM156 on MIAM-00133, after promotion from staging  
**Primary inputs:** `HANDOFF-PDU-CAPTURE-20260927.md` and `2026-09-17 Server Architecture: what Ethernet port and what power port are connected to what asset.xlsx`

---

## 1. Purpose

This document defines the next work for the PDU Manager after source capture, repository reconstruction, clean-room staging deployment, automated testing, and rollback validation.

The immediate objective is to move from the current arrangement—VM154 as production and VM156 as a Git-linked mock/staging instance—to a controlled Git-managed production deployment on VM156.

The intended end state is:

> **GitHub is the normal source of truth for the PDU Manager application and PDU configuration. Changes merge to `main`, an approved deployment automatically updates VM156, and production no longer depends on hand-edited files on a VM.**

The current server-architecture spreadsheet is authoritative for the initial hardware/outlet mapping imported into Git. After that import, the GitHub repository becomes authoritative unless Jordan explicitly supplies a newer architecture spreadsheet or other authoritative mapping.

---

# 2. Decisions Now Resolved

The following decisions supersede unresolved questions in Future Work v1.

| Topic | Decision |
|---|---|
| Current production VM | VM154 remains untouched until cutover is proven. |
| Target production VM | Promote VM156 to production. |
| VM154 after cutover | Set `onboot=0`, shut it down, and retain it temporarily as fallback. Do not delete it during initial cutover. |
| VM156 after cutover | Set `onboot=1` and make it the active PDU Manager production VM. |
| Deployment source | GitHub repository. |
| Deployment target | VM156. |
| Deployment approval | Human approval required by default. |
| Emergency override | An authorized human/admin may bypass the normal approval gate when necessary; the override must be auditable. |
| Post-approval behavior | After code is merged to `main` and production approval is granted, deployment may proceed automatically to VM156. |
| AI deployment authority | An AI agent may execute the deployment after the human GitHub approval or authorized override. |
| Outlet labels/configuration | Stored in Git-managed configuration; the UI does not need to edit hardware mapping. |
| Current hardware source of truth | The supplied server-architecture spreadsheet. |
| Future hardware source of truth | GitHub after the spreadsheet is reconciled into Git, unless Jordan provides a newer authoritative source. |
| Emergency local root login | Keep permanently. |
| LDAP | Keep/use LDAP authentication for normal employee access. |
| TLS/certificate modernization | Not required in this sprint. Keep the working TLS setup; revisit later if useful. |
| Formal PDU Manager RTO/RPO | Not required here; broader backup strategy handles disaster recovery. |

---

# 3. Authoritative KVM / PDU Mapping to Reconcile into Git

The supplied architecture spreadsheet establishes the following relevant assets:

## MIAM-00172

**Authoritative asset title:**

`MIAM-00172 - JetKVM Hardware Console`

Relevant spreadsheet mapping:

- Asset: `MIAM-00172`
- PDU: `MIAM-00153`
- PDU outlet: `12`
- Network identity listed in the MAC lookup: `10.0.10.172`
- Function: JetKVM hardware console

The spreadsheet also contains an operational note that JetKVM/TESmart keyboard compatibility requires the JetKVM HID `no_out_endpoint=1` behavior and references the persistent watchdog used to enforce it. That is hardware-console context and should remain documented, but it does not belong in the displayed PDU outlet label.

## MIAM-00182

**Authoritative asset title:**

`MIAM-00182 - TESmart 16-Port HDMI KVM Switch`

Relevant spreadsheet mapping:

- Asset: `MIAM-00182`
- PDU: `MIAM-00153`
- PDU outlet: `24`
- Ethernet switch: `MIAM-00120`
- Ethernet switch port: `24`
- Function: 16-port HDMI KVM switch

## Required Git-managed production labels

The PDU Manager configuration should therefore use:

```text
PDU MIAM-00153 / Outlet 12
MIAM-00172 - JetKVM Hardware Console

PDU MIAM-00153 / Outlet 24
MIAM-00182 - TESmart 16-Port HDMI KVM Switch
```

These values supersede the stale production-era labels documented in the source-capture handoff.

---

# 4. Important Constraint: VM156 Is Not Production Yet

The handoff confirms that VM156 currently runs the **mock backend** and was intentionally isolated from physical PDU actuation.

Therefore:

> **Do not simply shut down VM154 and declare VM156 production.**

VM156 must first be promoted from staging/mock mode to a validated production configuration that uses the real PDU backend, production authentication, production secrets, correct hardware mapping, and production monitoring.

Until the promotion passes all non-destructive checks, VM154 remains the production fallback.

---

# 5. Next Sprint Objective

## Sprint objective

**Promote VM156 from Git-linked staging to the Git-managed production PDU Manager, then safely retire VM154 from active service.**

The sprint is complete only when:

1. Git contains the authoritative PDU/KVM mapping.
2. VM156 can run against the real PDU backend.
3. VM156 uses the required LDAP and emergency-local authentication paths.
4. Production deployment from GitHub to VM156 is automated behind a human approval gate.
5. A rollback is proven.
6. Non-actuating production validation succeeds.
7. Monitoring is redirected to VM156.
8. VM156 is configured to start automatically.
9. VM154 is set not to start automatically and is powered off as fallback.
10. Routine manual editing on production is retired.

---

# 6. Phase A — Reconcile Git Configuration with the Architecture Spreadsheet

## FW-001 — Update Git-managed hardware mapping

**Priority:** P0  
**Status:** Required before VM156 promotion

The next AI agent should compare the repository's current configuration examples/defaults to the supplied architecture spreadsheet and update the Git-managed mapping.

At minimum update the KVM-related entries to:

```text
MIAM-00153 outlet 12 = MIAM-00172 - JetKVM Hardware Console
MIAM-00153 outlet 24 = MIAM-00182 - TESmart 16-Port HDMI KVM Switch
```

### Required work

1. Locate the configuration source used by deployment.
2. Confirm whether labels are stored directly in `config.json`, generated from another file, or templated by deployment scripts.
3. Update the Git-managed configuration.
4. Update matching documentation and requirements.
5. Update tests that encode the old labels.
6. Update VM156 configuration through the normal deployment/configuration mechanism—not by ad-hoc file editing where avoidable.
7. Record that the 2026-09-17 architecture spreadsheet was the source used for this reconciliation.

### Acceptance criteria

A repository search for the stale KVM labels returns no active configuration or active documentation references except historical handoff material where preservation is intentional.

---

## FW-002 — Define the long-term mapping source of truth

After this spreadsheet reconciliation:

- the **GitHub repository** becomes the authoritative mapping for the PDU Manager;
- a newer spreadsheet supplied by Jordan may supersede Git and trigger a reconciliation change;
- imported mapping changes must be reviewed before production deployment;
- hardware mapping should not be editable casually through the production UI.

Document this policy in `AGENTS.md`, `CURRENT_STATE.md`, or the appropriate repository governance document.

---

# 7. Phase B — Convert VM156 from Mock Staging to Production-Capable Deployment

## FW-003 — Preserve mock mode for testing

The mock backend is valuable and must remain supported by the codebase.

Do not remove it when promoting VM156.

The application should support an explicit backend selection such as:

```text
PDU_BACKEND=mock
PDU_BACKEND=real
```

or the repository's existing equivalent.

CI must continue to use mock mode only.

---

## FW-004 — Configure VM156 for the real PDU backend

**Priority:** P0

Create a production configuration for VM156 that points to the actual Tripp Lite / PowerAlert-controlled PDUs.

The current known PDUs are:

- `MIAM-00151`
- `MIAM-00152`
- `MIAM-00153`

### Requirements

- Production credentials must not be committed to Git.
- Real backend activation must happen only on VM156's production deployment path.
- CI and generic PR tests must remain unable to reach physical PDUs.
- Read-only state discovery should be used for validation before any live actuation is considered.

---

## FW-005 — Migrate production secrets to VM156

VM156 will require the production-equivalent secret set currently used by VM154.

The next agent must identify the existing deployment mechanism for secrets and securely provision VM156.

Expected categories include:

- PDU / PowerAlert credentials,
- LDAP service/bind credentials if applicable,
- application/session secret,
- emergency-local login credentials,
- TLS private material if VM156 terminates HTTPS itself.

### Safety rule

Never commit real secrets, passwords, private keys, decoded Base64 credentials, or production `.env` contents to GitHub.

---

# 8. Phase C — Authentication on VM156

## FW-006 — Preserve LDAP employee login

LDAP is already part of the captured production architecture and should remain the normal employee authentication mechanism on VM156.

The production cutover must verify:

- LDAP login succeeds,
- expected PDU roles/groups are recognized,
- unauthorized users remain blocked,
- temporary LDAP failure does not corrupt local state.

The captured system used LLDAP on `10.0.20.101:3890`.

### Clarification

The earlier question about "LDAP encryption" was about encrypting traffic between PDU Manager and the LDAP server using StartTLS/LDAPS. That security improvement is **not required for this cutover**. It can be evaluated separately later.

---

## FW-007 — Preserve emergency local root web login

**Decision:** Permanent feature.

Requirements:

- It must continue to function if LDAP is unavailable.
- Credentials must remain secret and outside Git.
- Use must be auditable.
- Normal employee use should continue through LDAP.
- CI must not contain or need the real emergency credential.

---

# 9. Phase D — GitHub-to-VM156 Deployment Pipeline

## What the "GitHub Actions production runner" means

A GitHub Actions runner is simply the machine that receives the approved deployment job from GitHub and performs the deployment to VM156.

The user-facing requirement is straightforward:

> **After a change is merged to `main` and a human approves production deployment, GitHub should automatically deploy that approved version to VM156.**

The exact runner implementation is an infrastructure detail and does not need to complicate normal use.

---

## FW-008 — Provide a least-privilege deployment runner

**Implementation direction:** Create a small dedicated deployment runner VM or LXC on the Marion management network, preferably on MIAM-00133 or another appropriate management host.

It should have only the access required to:

- receive GitHub Actions jobs,
- fetch verified release artifacts,
- connect to VM156,
- deploy/restart the PDU Manager,
- perform health checks,
- trigger rollback.

It should **not** be granted broad administrative access to unrelated infrastructure.

If the agent finds an equally secure simpler deployment architecture already present in the repository, it may use it and document the choice.

---

## FW-009 — Protected production deployment

Create/complete a GitHub `production` environment.

Normal path:

```text
feature branch
    ↓
PR / CI
    ↓
merge to main
    ↓
build immutable release
    ↓
WAIT FOR HUMAN APPROVAL
    ↓
approved deployment job
    ↓
VM156
    ↓
health verification
    ↓
success OR automatic rollback
```

### Approval policy

- Human approval is required by default.
- An authorized human/admin may use an emergency override/bypass when needed.
- Bypass events must be visible in GitHub/deployment history.
- After approval or authorized override, an AI agent may perform the deployment automatically.

---

## FW-010 — Production deployment workflow

The production workflow should:

1. only deploy code merged to `main`,
2. require passing CI,
3. produce/reference an immutable release artifact,
4. verify artifact integrity,
5. require the production approval gate,
6. preserve `/etc/pdu-control` production configuration/secrets unless an explicit reviewed config migration is part of the release,
7. preserve `/var/lib/pdu-control` runtime state,
8. preserve audit history,
9. install/update application code,
10. restart only required PDU Manager services,
11. run non-actuating health checks,
12. automatically rollback on failed health checks,
13. record the deployed Git commit/release identifier.

### Hard safety rule

**Never verify a deployment by sending ON, OFF, REBOOT, Cycle Load, or other power-changing commands to a physical outlet.**

---

# 10. Phase E — VM156 Production Validation

## FW-011 — Validate VM156 before cutover

Before VM154 is shut down, verify all of the following on VM156.

### Application

- PDU Manager service starts cleanly.
- nginx/HTTPS serves the application.
- application health endpoint succeeds.
- service survives an application restart.
- service survives a VM reboot.

### Authentication

- LDAP employee login works.
- role mapping works.
- emergency local root web login works.

### Configuration

- Git-managed PDU labels match the architecture spreadsheet.
- protected outlet rules match the intended physical assets.
- PDU inventory matches production expectations.

### Real backend — read-only validation

- application can establish the expected backend connection,
- outlet state reads succeed,
- PDU state refresh works,
- backend errors are reported correctly,
- no power-changing action is executed during cutover validation.

### Logging/state

- audit logs are writable,
- idempotency state location is writable,
- pending-reboot handling is sane,
- application restart does not lose required state.

### Monitoring

- external monitoring can reach VM156,
- expected health/login endpoints respond,
- no stale monitor continues treating VM154 as the only active service after cutover.

---

# 11. Phase F — Production Cutover VM154 → VM156

## FW-012 — Cut over production

Once VM156 passes the production-capable validation:

1. Record the current running release on VM154.
2. Capture a final VM154 backup/snapshot according to the existing backup strategy.
3. Capture current VM154 configuration and state needed for fallback.
4. Confirm VM156 is running the approved Git release.
5. Confirm VM156 is using the **real** PDU backend.
6. Confirm LDAP and emergency local login.
7. Confirm read-only PDU status retrieval.
8. Redirect operational use/monitoring to VM156.
9. Set VM156 `onboot=1` in Proxmox.
10. Verify VM156 remains healthy after a controlled reboot.
11. Set VM154 `onboot=0`.
12. Shut down VM154.
13. Keep VM154 available, powered off, as temporary rollback/fallback.

### Do not delete VM154 during this sprint

Retain it until VM156 has operated successfully in production for an agreed observation period.

A future cleanup task may archive/delete VM154 after confidence is established and backups are confirmed.

---

## FW-013 — Production address / monitoring transition

The current handoff references production at `https://10.0.20.154/` and staging at `https://10.0.20.156/`.

For the initial cutover, treat VM156 as the production endpoint unless there is an existing DNS/VIP mechanism that allows the service identity to remain stable.

The agent must update:

- external monitoring,
- operator documentation,
- health-check configuration,
- deployment documentation,
- any internal links that incorrectly assume VM154 is active production.

Do not reassign IP addresses or introduce a VIP merely for cosmetic continuity unless explicitly planned and tested.

---

# 12. Phase G — Retire Ad-Hoc Production Editing

## FW-014 — Git-first production operations

After successful cutover:

- application code changes originate in Git,
- PDU mapping/config changes originate in Git,
- PR + CI remains the normal review mechanism,
- approved `main` releases deploy to VM156,
- direct source editing on VM156 is emergency-only.

### Break-glass rule

If an emergency direct edit is required:

1. document why,
2. preserve the pre-change state,
3. make the minimum required change,
4. verify safely,
5. immediately reconcile/back-port the exact change to Git,
6. return production to a Git-known state.

---

# 13. Remaining Hardening / Future Work

These items remain useful but should not block the VM156 production migration unless implementation reveals a dependency.

## FW-015 — Audit-log rotation

The capture handoff reports that audit logs are not rotated.

Implement safe rotation for:

- `/var/log/pdu-control/audit.log`
- `/var/log/pdu-control/audit.log.jsonl`

**Retention period:** still TBD. Until an explicit retention policy is selected, do not create an aggressive deletion policy.

---

## FW-016 — Remove unused `SNMP_*` secrets

Confirm no active code/deployment dependency, then remove unused variables in a small reviewed PR.

---

## FW-017 — Remove legacy `app_direct.py`

The handoff identifies this as pre-V3 code not imported by the live app.

Delete only after current production parity is verified on VM156.

---

## FW-018 — Configuration schema validation

Add formal validation for the Git-managed PDU configuration before deployment.

Validate at minimum:

- valid PDU IDs,
- valid outlet numbers,
- no unintended duplicates,
- required asset labels,
- protected-outlet rules,
- supported config/schema version.

A malformed configuration must fail before the production service is restarted.

---

## FW-019 — Protection-to-asset consistency tests

Protection rules should be tested against the physical asset mapping so that renaming/moving an asset does not silently leave protection applied to the wrong equipment.

---

## FW-020 — Dry-run / explain mode

Future administrative feature:

Show what an action **would** do without sending the command to hardware:

- PDU,
- outlet,
- asset,
- requested action,
- protection status,
- required override/acknowledgement,
- selected backend command.

This would be particularly useful for AI-agent operation and safety review.

---

## FW-021 — Deployment/version visibility

Future quality-of-life improvement:

Expose the deployed application version or Git commit SHA in an authenticated admin location or footer so an operator can easily answer:

> "Which Git version is actually running on VM156?"

---

## FW-022 — LDAP transport encryption review

LDAP employee authentication remains required.

A later security-hardening sprint may evaluate StartTLS/LDAPS for encrypted PDU Manager ↔ LLDAP traffic.

This is **not required for the VM156 cutover**.

---

## FW-023 — TLS certificate modernization — deferred

"Managed TLS" means replacing the manually/self-signed HTTPS certificate with one issued and renewed through a managed internal certificate process.

There is no current requirement to do this.

Keep the existing working HTTPS approach during the production migration. Revisit only if certificate warnings, hostname management, or renewal burden justify it.

---

# AGENTS.md

Instructions for autonomous and semi-autonomous coding agents working in this repository.

## 1. Authority model

Humans set product intent, approve scope, approve significant decisions, and approve merges.

Agents may:

- inspect the repository;
- propose a short plan;
- implement approved requirements;
- create or update automated tests;
- update documentation affected by the change;
- add newly discovered ideas to `FUTURE_WORK.md`;
- draft ADRs and TDRs;
- run non-destructive validation;
- prepare commits and pull requests.

Agents must not:

- silently add product scope;
- promote future work into approved requirements without human approval;
- accept their own newly invented significant architecture decision;
- merge their own work under the current operating model;
- commit directly to `main`/production branches;
- use destructive commands, rotate secrets, alter production data, change permissions, or modify infrastructure without explicit human approval;
- hide failed validation, security concerns, or unresolved trade-offs.

## 2. Read before planning

For every meaningful change, read the relevant parts of:

1. `README.md`
2. `REQUIREMENTS.md`
3. `SPRINT.md`
4. `docs/ARCHITECTURE.md`
5. relevant files in `docs/adr/`
6. relevant files in `docs/tdr/`
7. `FUTURE_WORK.md` when scope or follow-up work is involved

Repository-local instructions and approved requirements are the primary source of truth.

## 3. Requirement discipline

Implementation work should trace to one or more `REQ-###` entries.

Before editing, summarize:

- the requirement IDs being implemented;
- the acceptance criteria;
- the files/components likely to change;
- assumptions or ambiguities;
- the validation that will prove the change works.

If the requested work is not covered by an approved requirement, do one of the following:

- ask for human approval to add/modify the requirement; or
- record the idea in `FUTURE_WORK.md` if it is not part of the current scope.

Do not invent scope merely because it seems useful.

## 4. Planning and execution

Before meaningful edits, provide a short implementation plan. Keep the plan proportional to the change.

During execution:

- work in a dedicated branch/worktree/sandbox;
- keep the diff focused;
- follow the existing architecture and project conventions;
- prefer readable, conventional code over clever code;
- avoid unrelated cleanup;
- preserve backward compatibility unless an approved requirement or ADR says otherwise;
- update affected documentation in the same change.

If the implementation grows materially beyond the approved plan, stop and surface the scope increase.

## 5. Significant decisions and ADRs

Create or update an ADR when a change makes a significant, durable decision about architecture, major dependencies, data/storage, external integrations, security boundaries, deployment model, scalability approach, or another choice that future maintainers are likely to ask "why did we choose this?"

### Human-directed decision

If a human explicitly made the decision, the agent may record it as `Status: Accepted`. The ADR must still be called out in the pull request so the human sees what was recorded.

### Agent-originated decision

If the agent concludes a significant decision is needed, create a draft ADR with `Status: Proposed` and surface it for human approval. Do not treat it as approved until a human accepts it.

Use `docs/adr/TEMPLATE.md`.

## 6. Technical debt and TDRs

Create a TDR when a change intentionally leaves known technical debt such as a shortcut, workaround, temporary design, missing test, manual step, or deferred reliability/security/performance/maintainability improvement.

Use `docs/tdr/TEMPLATE.md` and keep the record concise.

An agent may propose a TDR, but the human reviewer should confirm that the debt is acceptable and that the owner is appropriate.

## 7. Validation

Use the exact validation commands documented by the project once the implementation stack is selected.

Run all checks relevant to the change, such as:

- formatting;
- lint/static analysis;
- type checking where applicable;
- unit tests;
- integration tests;
- end-to-end tests where applicable;
- build/package validation;
- security/secret/dependency checks;
- deployment or readiness checks when relevant.

Report exactly what passed, failed, or was not run. Never imply that a check passed if it was skipped or unavailable.

If expected commands are not documented, surface that as an agent-readiness gap instead of guessing silently.

## 8. Security and data handling

- Do not commit secrets, credentials, private keys, access tokens, or sensitive customer/company data.
- Treat generated shell commands, migrations, dependencies, and configuration changes as untrusted until reviewed.
- Do not weaken authentication, authorization, encryption, logging, isolation, or monitoring merely to make a task easier.
- Surface security-sensitive behavior for explicit human review.

## 9. Git and pull requests

Startup Teams Git policy applies unless a stricter project policy is documented.

- Use a dedicated branch; direct commits to `main`/production branches are prohibited.
- Branch format: `type/ticket-short-description` (for example, `feat/AGR-24-bnpl-checkout`).
- Commit format: `type(scope): short description (#issue-id)`.
- Commit scope is required.
- Every commit must reference the related issue/ticket.
- Keep commits and PRs small enough to review.
- Required validation must pass before merge.
- A human reviewer must approve before merge under the current operating model.

Every PR should state:

- feature / requirement IDs;
- what changed and why;
- files/components changed;
- validation performed and results;
- known risks or limitations;
- documentation changes;
- ADRs/TDRs added or changed;
- future-work items discovered;
- reviewer focus areas.

## 10. Stop and ask for human judgment when

- requirements conflict or are materially ambiguous;
- a new significant ADR decision is required;
- credentials or restricted access are required;
- a destructive action is needed;
- tests fail unexpectedly and the cause is not understood;
- security-sensitive behavior changes;
- production data or infrastructure would be changed;
- the diff grows substantially beyond the planned scope;
- a future-work idea would need to become approved product scope.

## 11. Handoff standard

At the end of a work session or before handing work to another agent/human, provide:

1. requirement IDs addressed;
2. concise summary of changes;
3. files changed;
4. validation commands and results;
5. known risks / unresolved questions;
6. ADR/TDR status;
7. future-work items discovered;
8. recommended next action.

## 12. PDU Manager production safety additions

This application can control real electrical power. Treat production interactions as high-consequence infrastructure operations.

Agents MUST NOT perform real PDU ON/OFF/REBOOT/CYCLE operations as tests unless a human explicitly authorizes the exact outlet/action for that session.

During normal development, source capture, CI, staging, or deployment verification:

- use mocks/fixtures for actuation behavior;
- prefer read-only outlet-state checks when technically enforceable;
- do not reboot VM154 or `MIAM-00133` merely to apply application changes;
- restart only the application service(s) when a service restart is sufficient;
- do not modify production outlet labels, presets, protection/lockout settings, or credentials as an incidental test;
- never place PDU/LDAP/TLS credentials in Git;
- preserve protection of critical power targets;
- treat any change that could remove a safety interlock as security/safety sensitive and stop for human review.

The production endpoint is currently `https://10.0.20.154/` on VM154 hosted by `MIAM-00133`. This location is an approved deployment target, not permission for unrestricted infrastructure changes.

{"content": "1|#!/usr/bin/env bash\n2|# ============================================================================\n3|# deploy/install.sh — clean-VM installer for the PDU Manager (Debian 12)\n4|#\n5|# Usage:   sudo ./deploy/install.sh [path/to/pdu-manager-<sha>.tar.gz]\n6|#          (without an artifact, installs from the current repo tree)\n7|#\n8|# Idempotent. Safe to re-run. Never touches /etc/pdu-control/config.json or\n9|# secrets.env if they already exist (mutable site config preserved, REQ-015).\n10|#\n11|# Staging mode:  PDU_STAGING=1 ./deploy/install.sh ...   → mock PDU backend\n12|#                (sets PDU_BACKEND=mock in the service environment)\n13|# ============================================================================\n14|set -Eeuo pipefail\n15|\n16|APP_USER=\"pducontrol\"\n17|OPT_DIR=\"/opt/pdu-control\"\n18|ETC_DIR=\"/etc/pdu-control\"\n19|LOG_DIR=\"/var/log/pdu-control\"\n20|STATE_DIR=\"/var/lib/pdu-control\"\n21|BACKUP_DIR=\"/var/backups/pdu-control\"\n22|STAGING=\"${PDU_STAGING:-0}\"\n23|ARTIFACT=\"${1:-}\"\n24|\n25|if [[ $EUID -ne 0 ]]; then\n26|  echo \"FATAL: run as root (sudo).\" >&2\n27|  exit 1\n28|fi\n29|\n30|echo \"== [1/9] OS prerequisites ==\"\n31|export DEBIAN_FRONTEND=noninteractive\n32|apt-get update -qq\n33|apt-get install -y -qq python3 python3-venv python3-pip openssh-client nginx nftables openssl ca-certificates rsync >/dev/null\n34|\n35|echo \"== [2/9] Service identity ==\"\n36|id -u \"$APP_USER\" >/dev/null 2>&1 || useradd --system --home \"$OPT_DIR\" --shell /usr/sbin/nologin \"$APP_USER\"\n37|\n38|echo \"== [3/9] Directories ==\"\n39|mkdir -p \"$OPT_DIR\" \"$LOG_DIR\" \"$STATE_DIR\" \"$BACKUP_DIR\"\n40|touch \"$LOG_DIR/audit.log\" \"$LOG_DIR/audit.log.jsonl\"\n41|\n42|echo \"== [4/9] Release payload ==\"\n43|if [[ -n \"$ARTIFACT\" ]]; then\n44|  SHA_DIR=\"$(mktemp -d /tmp/pdu-release-XXXXXX)\"\n45|  tar -xzf \"$ARTIFACT\" -C \"$SHA_DIR\"\n46|  PAYLOAD=\"$(find \"$SHA_DIR\" -maxdepth 1 -type d -name 'pdu-manager-*' | head -1)\"\n47|  RELEASE_SHA=\"$(basename \"$PAYLOAD\" | sed 's/^pdu-manager-//')\"\n48|  echo \"installing artifact release sha=$RELEASE_SHA\"\n49|  RELEASE_DIR=\"$OPT_DIR/releases/$RELEASE_SHA\"\n50|  mkdir -p \"$OPT_DIR/releases\" \"$RELEASE_DIR\"\n51|  rsync -a --delete \"$PAYLOAD/app/\" \"$RELEASE_DIR/app/\" 2>/dev/null || cp -a \"$PAYLOAD/app\" \"$RELEASE_DIR/app\"\n52|  cp -a \"$PAYLOAD/requirements.txt\" \"$RELEASE_DIR/\"\n53|  cp -a \"$PAYLOAD/deploy\" \"$RELEASE_DIR/\" 2>/dev/null || true\n54|  ln -sfn \"$RELEASE_DIR\" \"$OPT_DIR/current\"\n55|  APP_SRC=\"$RELEASE_DIR/app\"\n56|  REQ=\"$RELEASE_DIR/requirements.txt\"\n57|else\n58|  REPO_ROOT=\"$(cd \"$(dirname \"$0\")/..\" && pwd)\"\n59|  APP_SRC=\"$REPO_ROOT/app\"\n60|  REQ=\"$REPO_ROOT/requirements.txt\"\n61|  ln -sfn \"$REPO_ROOT\" \"$OPT_DIR/current\" 2>/dev/null || true\n62|fi\n63|\n64|echo \"== [5/9] venv + dependencies ==\"\n65|python3 -m venv \"$OPT_DIR/venv\"\n66|\"$OPT_DIR/venv/bin/pip\" install --quiet --upgrade pip\n67|\"$OPT_DIR/venv/bin/pip\" install --quiet -r \"$REQ\"\n68|\n69|echo \"== [6/9] App code into /opt/pdu-control ==\"\n70|# release dir keeps the immutable copy; the live tree is what the venv+unit use\n71|rsync -a \"$APP_SRC/\" \"$OPT_DIR/\" --exclude 'venv' --exclude '__pycache__'\n72|chown -R \"$APP_USER:$APP_USER\" \"$OPT_DIR\" \"$LOG_DIR\" \"$STATE_DIR\"\n73|chown root:\"$APP_USER\" \"$OPT_DIR\" 2>/dev/null || true\n74|\n75|echo \"== [7/9] Configuration (preserved if present) ==\"\n76|mkdir -p \"$ETC_DIR\"\n77|if [[ ! -f \"$ETC_DIR/config.json\" ]]; then\n78|  SRC_CFG=\"$(dirname \"$0\")/../config/examples/config.example.json\"\n79|  [[ -n \"$ARTIFACT\" ]] && SRC_CFG=\"$(find \"$(dirname \"$PAYLOAD\")\" -name 'config.example.json' | head -1)\"\n80|  install -o root -g \"$APP_USER\" -m 640 \"$SRC_CFG\" \"$ETC_DIR/config.json\"\n81|  echo \"  installed example config -> $ETC_DIR/config.json\"\n82|else\n83|  echo \"  config.json EXISTS — left untouched\"\n84|fi\n85|if [[ ! -f \"$ETC_DIR/secrets.env\" ]]; then\n86|  echo \"  NOTE: $ETC_DIR/secrets.env missing — create it from deploy/examples/secrets.env.example\" >&2\n87|  echo \"        (staging with PDU_STAGING=1 still needs WEB_PASS for the emergency path)\" >&2\n88|fi\n89|if [[ \"$STAGING\" == \"1\" && ! -f \"$ETC_DIR/secrets.env\" ]]; }\n93|  cat > \"$ETC_DIR/secrets.env\" <<EOF\n94|# STAGING-ONLY throwaway values (install.sh generated; mock backend active)\n95|PDU_USER_B64=$(_b64 stage-user)\n96|PDU_PASS_B64=$(_b64 stage-pass-not-a-real-secret)\n97|WEB_USER_B64=$(_b64 root)\n98|WEB_PASS_B64=$(_b64 staging-emergency-change-on-first-run)\n99|LDAP_SERVICE_USER_B64=$(_b64 svc)\n100|LDAP_SERVICE_PASS_B64=$(_b64 svc-not-a-real-secret)\n101|EOF\n102|  chown root:\"$APP_USER\" \"$ETC_DIR/secrets.env\"; The captured code is\n5|# intentionally unmodified (this is a capture, not a rewrite), so tests\n6|# redirect those paths using a sitecustomize-style import hook: we create a\n7|# temp fixture tree and patch builtins.open only for those exact paths.\n8|\n9|import base64\n10|import json\n11|import os\n12|import sys\n13|import tempfile\n14|from pathlib import Path\n15|\n16|REPO_ROOT = Path(__file__).resolve().parents[1]\n17|APP_DIR = REPO_ROOT / \"app\"\n18|sys.path.insert(0, str(APP_DIR))\n19|\n20|TMP = Path(tempfile.mkdtemp(prefix=\"pdu-capture-test-\"))\n21|\n22|_config = TMP / \"config.json\"\n23|_secrets = TMP / \"secrets.env\"\n24|\n25|import shutil\n26|shutil.copy(REPO_ROOT / \"config\" / \"examples\" / \"config.example.json\", _config)\n27|\n28|_b64 = lambda s: base64.b64encode(s.encode()).decode()\n29|_secrets.write_text(\n30|    \"PDU_USER_B64=%s\\nPDU_PASS_B64=%s\\n\"\n31|    \"SNMP_VERSION_B64=%s\\nSNMP_RO_B64=%s\\nSNMP_RW_B64=%s\\n\"\n32|    \"WEB_USER_B64=%s\\nWEB_PASS_B64=%s\\n\"\n33|    \"LDAP_SERVICE_USER_B64=%s\\nLDAP_SERVICE_PASS_B64=%s\\n\"\n34|    % (_b64(\"testuser\"), _b64(\"testpass\"), _b64(\"2c\"), _b64(\"ro\"), _b64(\"rw\"),\n35|       _b64(\"root\"), _b64(\"test-emergency\"), _b64(\"svc\"), _b64(\"svcpass\"))\n36|)\n37|\n38|_log = TMP / \"logs\"; Hardware/outlet mapping authority (FW-002, established 2026-09-27)\n+\n+The **GitHub repository is the authoritative source of truth for PDU/outlet/hardware mapping** (asset labels, outlet numbering, protected-outlet rules, PDU inventory) once the initial reconciliation from the 2026-09-17 server-architecture spreadsheet (FW-001) has merged.\n+\n+Rules:\n+\n+1. **Mapping changes originate in Git** — a PR that updates `config/examples/config.example.json` (and any related docs/tests), reviewed through normal CI, is the only sanctioned path.\n+2. **A newer spreadsheet or mapping supplied explicitly by Jordan supersedes Git** and triggers a reviewed reconciliation PR — not an unmanaged production edit.\n+3. **Production UI must not be used to casually edit hardware mapping.** After the VM156 cutover, mapping changes flow Git → approved release → VM156. {"output": "docs/adr/:\n0001-use-markdown-and-mermaid-for-architecture-documentation.md\n0002-github-source-of-truth-and-vm154-production.md\n0003-cicd-deployment-model.md\nTEMPLATE.md\n\ndocs/tdr/:\n0001-production-vm-not-yet-reproducible-from-github.md\nTEMPLATE.md\n# ADR-0002: GitHub Source of Truth with VM154 as the Production Target\n\n**Status:** Accepted  \n**Date:** 2026-09-26\n\n## Context\n\nThe PDU Manager is already running in production on VM154 hosted by `MIAM-00133`, but the running VM predates a complete source-controlled project/deployment workflow. The system owner has directed that the live PDU Manager code be captured into `startupteams/pdu-marion-ia-usa-project-framework` and that future modifications be delivered through a CI/CD pipeline after validating that the repository can recreate the current service.\n\nA mutable production VM should not remain the only practical source of application code because that creates recovery, review, traceability, and maintainability risk.\n\n## Decision\n\nAfter live-source capture, secret removal, clean-environment reproduction, and human-reviewed parity validation:\n\n1. the GitHub repository will become the authoritative source for PDU Manager application code and deployment assets;\n2. VM154 on `MIAM-00133` will remain the production target unless a later human-approved decision changes it;\n3. future application changes will normally flow through branch -> CI -> pull request -> human review -> merge -> staging -> approved production deployment;\n4. production code should not normally be edited in place outside this workflow;\n5. deployment validation will use non-actuating/read-only checks and will not power-cycle real hardware as an automated test.\n\nThis ADR does **not** select the exact packaging technology or self-hosted runner placement. Those decisions depend on the live stack discovered during capture and require separate review if significant.\n\n## Consequences\n\n- Production releases become traceable to Git commits/artifacts.\n- Disaster recovery improves because the application can be reconstructed from source plus externalized secrets/configuration.\n- Changes gain automated validation and human review.\n- A staging/reproducibility environment becomes necessary.\n- Secrets and mutable site configuration must be separated from code.", "exit_code": 0, "error": null}

{"content": "30|\n31|# Backend selection: PDU_BACKEND=mock (CI/staging) swaps the state-read and\n32|# control paths to the in-repo mock driver. In mock mode the real SSH driver\n33|# is never imported, so no hardware can be reached at all (REQ-010).\n34|if os.environ.get(\"PDU_BACKEND\", \"\").lower() == \"mock\":\n35|    import mock_pdu_backend as pdu_direct\n36|else:\n37|    import pdu_ssh_direct as pdu_direct\n38|\n39|CONFIG_FILE = \"/etc/pdu-control/config.json\"\n40|SECRETS_FILE = \"/etc/pdu-control/secrets.env\"\n41|AUDIT_FILE = \"/var/log/pdu-control/audit.log\"\n42|AUDIT_DB_FILE = \"/var/lib/pdu-control/audit.sqlite3\"\n43|WORKER_FILE = \"/opt/pdu-control/pdu_worker.py\"\n44|PENDING_FILE = \"/var/lib/pdu-control/pending_reboot.json\"\n45|PYTHON_BIN = \"/opt/pdu-control/venv/bin/python\"\n46|COMMAND_TIMEOUT_SECONDS = 60\n47|MAX_BATCH_ITEMS = 72\n48|PROTECTED_RECOVERY_ATTEMPTS = 2  # small configurable limit (V3 14.3)\n49|SELF_HOST_SETTLE_SECONDS = 8  # seconds after cycle confirm before skipping verify\n50|\n51|with open(CONFIG_FILE, \"r\", encoding=\"utf-8\") as handle:\n52|    CONFIG = json.load(handle)\n53|\n54|\n55|def load_secrets():\n56|    values = {}\n57|    with open(SECRETS_FILE, \"r\", encoding=\"utf-8\") as handle:\n58|        for raw in handle:\n59|            line = raw.strip()\n60|            if not line or \"=\" not in line:\n61|                continue\n62|            key, value = line.split(\"=\", 1)\n63|            values[key] = value.strip()\n64|    return values\n65|\n66|\n67|RAW_SECRETS = load_secrets()\n68|\n69|\n70|def secret(name):\n71|    encoded = RAW_SECRETS.get(f\"{name}_B64\", \"\")\n72|    if not encoded:\n73|        return \"\"\n74|    return base64.b64decode(encoded).decode(\"utf-8\")\n75|\n76|\n77|# One gunicorn worker is required. Threads share these runtime structures.\n78|RUNTIME_LOCK = threading.RLock()\n79|PDU_RUNTIME = {}\n80|STATE_CACHE = {}\n81|COMPLETED_JOBS = {}  # job_id -> summary snapshot (last 200, in-process)\n82|WAITING_JOBS = defaultdict(list)  # ip -> list of (items, action, meta) waiting for lock\n83|WAITING_LOCK = threading.Lock()\n84|\n85|\n86|def new_runtime(ip):\n87|    return {\n88|        \"ip\": ip,\n89|        \"busy\": False,\n90|        \"job_id\": None,\n91|        \"action\": None,\n92|        \"started_at\": None,\n93|        \"command_started_at\": None,\n94|        \"deadline\": None,\n95|        \"current_outlet\": None,\n96|        \"current_label\": None,\n97|        \"queue_index\": 0,\n98|        \"queue_total\": 0,\n99|        \"message\": \"READY\",\n100|        \"last_status\": \"ready\",\n101|        \"last_message\": \"READY\",\n102|        \"last_completed_at\": None,\n103|        \"results\": [],\n104|    }\n105|", "total_lines": 767, "file_size": 27406, "truncated": true, "hint": "Use offset=105 to continue reading (showing 30-104 of 767 lines)", "is_binary": false, "is_image": false}

{"output": "def reconcile_pending_reboot():\n    pending = read_pending_reboot()\n    if not pending:\n        return\n    ip = pending[\"ip\"]\n    outlet = int(pending[\"outlet\"])\n    audit_write(\n        f'ip={ip} outlet={outlet} job={pending.get(\"job_id\")} '\n        f'actor={pending.get(\"actor\")} result=RECONCILE_START reason=\"{pending.get(\"reason\", \"\")}\"',\n        \"system\",\n        actor=\"system\",\n    )\n    try:\n        result = pdu_direct.read_state(ip, outlet)\n        state = result[\"state\"]\n        if state == \"ON\":\n            with RUNTIME_LOCK:\n                cached = STATE_CACHE.setdefault(ip, {})\n                cached[outlet] = {\"state\": \"ON\", \"controllable\": True}\n            audit_write(\n                f'ip={ip} outlet={outlet} job={pending.get(\"job_id\")} '\n                f'result=RECONCILE_SUCCESS final_state=ON',\n                \"system\",\n                actor=\"system\",\n            )\n        else:\n            # Expected final state ON was not observed: attempt recovery ON.\n            try:\n                run_control_process(ip, outlet, \"on\")\n                audit_write(\n                    f'ip={ip} outlet={outlet} job={pending.get(\"job_id\")} '\n                    f'result=RECONCILE_RECOVERY final_state=ON (was {state})',\n                    \"system\",\n                    actor=\"system\",\n                )\n            except Exception as exc:\n                audit_write(\n                    f'ip={ip} outlet={outlet} job={pending.get(\"job_id\")} '\n                    f'severity=HIGH result=RECONCILE_FAILED error=\"{exc}\"',\n                    \"system\",\n                    actor=\"system\",\n                )\n    except Exception as exc:\n        audit_write(\n            f'ip={ip} outlet={outlet} job={pending.get(\"job_id\")} '\n            f'severity=HIGH result=RECONCILE_UNVERIFIED error=\"{exc}\"',\n            \"system\",\n            actor=\"system\",\n        )\n    finally:\n        clear_pending_reboot()\n\n\ndef startup_tasks():", "exit_code": 0, "error": null}

{"output": "#!/usr/bin/env python3\n\"\"\"CLI wrapper the app subprocess-spawns for power operations.\n\nBackend selection: PDU_BACKEND=mock routes to the in-repo mock driver\n(CI/staging; never touches hardware). Default = real PowerAlert SSH driver.\n\"\"\"\nimport json\nimport os\nimport sys\n\n\ndef main():\n    if len(sys.argv) != 4:\n        raise SystemExit(\"Usage: pdu_worker.py <ip> <outlet> <on|off|reboot>\")\n\n    ip = sys.argv[1]\n    outlet = int(sys.argv[2])\n    action = sys.argv[3].lower()\n\n    if os.environ.get(\"PDU_BACKEND\", \"\").lower() == \"mock\":\n        import mock_pdu_backend as backend\n    else:\n        import pdu_ssh_direct as backend\n\n    try:\n        final_state = backend.control(ip, outlet, action)\n        print(json.dumps({\"ok\": True, \"final_state\": final_state}))\n    except Exception as exc:\n        print(json.dumps({\"ok\": False, \"error\": str(exc)}))\n        raise SystemExit(1)\n\n\nif __name__ == \"__main__\":\n    main()\n=== mock read_state ===\n#!/usr/bin/env python3\n\"\"\"Mock PDU backend for CI/staging — never touches real hardware.\n\nImplements the same function surface pdu_ssh_direct exposes and is used by\nthe app when PDU_BACKEND=mock (or when /etc/pdu-control/pdu_backend_mode\nsays 'mock'). The runtime imports the driver lazily via this selector, so a\nstaging install can run the FULL app without any PDU reachability.\n\nStates: outlets default to 'Off'; ON/OFF/REBOOT mutate an in-memory dict.\n\"\"\"\nimport threading\nimport time\n\n_STATES = {}          # ip -> {outlet: {\"state\": \"On\"|\"Off\", \"controllable\": True}}\n_LOCK = threading.RLock()\n\n\ndef _ensure(ip, outlets=24):\n    with _LOCK:\n        if ip not in _STATES:\n            _STATES[ip] = {i: {\"state\": \"Off\", \"controllable\": True} for i in range(1, outlets + 1)}\n        return _STATES[ip]\n\n\ndef connect(ip):\n    class _FakeChannel:\n        def close(self, force=False):\n            pass\n    return _FakeChannel()\n\n\ndef read_state(ip, outlet, verbose=False):\n    table = _ensure(ip)\n    with _LOCK:\n        entry = table[int(outlet)]\n        return {\"state\": entry[\"state\"].upper(), \"controllable\": True, \"screen\": \"MOCK\"}\n\n\ndef read_all_states(ip, verbose=False):\n    table = _ensure(ip)\n    with _LOCK:\n        return {int(k): dict(v) for k, v in table.items()}", "exit_code": 0, "error": null}

{"output": "#!/usr/bin/env python3\n\"\"\"Guest-exec helper for VM154 via PVE API (read-only capture operations).\n\nUsage:\n  vm154_exec.py \"command string\"\n\nHandles:\n- login via pve_api (LLDAP bot creds)\n- exec dispatch + status polling (exec-status is a GET with ?pid=)\n- base64 decode of out-data / err-data when agent returns encoded strings\n\"\"\"\nimport sys, json, base64, time\nsys.path.insert(0, \"/home/jordatech/.hermes/profiles/agent_stea004_entrepreneur/skills/devops/proxmox-cluster-infrastructure/scripts\")\nfrom pve_api import api\n\n\ndef guest_exec(cmd, timeout=90, node=\"miam-00133\", vmid=\"154\"):\n    b64 = base64.b64encode(cmd.encode()).decode()\n    st, d = api(\"POST\", f\"/api2/json/nodes/{node}/qemu/{vmid}/agent/exec\",\n                body={\"command\": [\"bash\", \"-lc\", f\"echo {b64} | base64 -d | bash\"]})\n    if st != 200 or not d.get(\"data\"):\n        return {\"exec_fail\": True, \"status\": st, \"resp\": d}\n    pid = d[\"data\"][\"pid\"]\n    t0 = time.time()\n    while time.time() - t0 < timeout:\n        time.sleep(3)\n        st2, d2 = api(\"GET\", f\"/api2/json/nodes/{node}/qemu/{vmid}/agent/exec-status?pid={pid}\")\n        res = d2.get(\"data\", {}) or {}\n        if res.get(\"exited\"):\n            out = res.get(\"out-data\") or \"\"\n            err = res.get(\"err-data\") or \"\"\n\n            def dec(s):\n                if not s:\n                    return \"\"\n                # agent may return plain text or base64; detect\n                try:\n                    return base64.b64decode(s, validate=True).decode()\n                except Exception:\n                    return s\n\n            text = dec(out)\n            if not text and out:\n                text = out\n            errtext = dec(err)\n            return {\"exitcode\": res.get(\"exitcode\"), \"out\": text, \"err\": errtext,\n                    \"truncated\": res.get(\"out-truncated\", 0)}\n    return {\"timeout\": True, \"pid\": pid}", "exit_code": 0, "error": null}

{"output": "#!/usr/bin/env bash\n# ============================================================================\n# deploy/build-release.sh — build an immutable PDU Manager release artifact\n#\n# Usage:   ./deploy/build-release.sh [GIT_SHA]\n# Output:  dist/pdu-manager-<sha>.tar.gz + dist/pdu-manager-<sha>.sha256\n#\n# The artifact contains ONLY: app code, deploy assets, docs, requirements.txt.\n# It contains NO secrets, NO config (config is external), NO state.\n# ============================================================================\nset -Eeuo pipefail\n\nSHA=\"${1:-$(git rev-parse --verify HEAD)}\"\nREPO_ROOT=\"$(cd \"$(dirname \"$0\")/..\" && pwd)\"\nDIST=\"$REPO_ROOT/dist\"\nSTAGE=\"$(mktemp -d /tmp/pdu-release-XXXXXX)\"\n\ntrap 'rm -rf \"$STAGE\"' EXIT\n\necho \"[build-release] source: repo@$SHA\"\n\n# --- verify clean-ish tree for the sha we ship -------------------------------\ngit -C \"$REPO_ROOT\" rev-parse --verify \"$SHA\" >/dev/null || {\n  echo \"FATAL: unknown git sha: $SHA\" >&2; exit 1;\n}\n\n# --- assemble staging tree ----------------------------------------------------\nmkdir -p \"$STAGE/pdu-manager-$SHA\"\nSTAGE_APP=\"$STAGE/pdu-manager-$SHA\"\ncp -a \"$REPO_ROOT/app\"            \"$STAGE_APP/app\"\ncp -a \"$REPO_ROOT/deploy\"         \"$STAGE_APP/deploy\"\ncp -a \"$REPO_ROOT/docs\"           \"$STAGE_APP/docs\"\ncp    \"$REPO_ROOT/requirements.txt\" \"$STAGE_APP/requirements.txt\"\ncp    \"$REPO_ROOT/config/examples/config.example.json\" \"$STAGE_APP/config.example.json\"\ncp    \"$REPO_ROOT/tests\"/*.py     \"$STAGE_APP/\" 2>/dev/null || true\ncp -a \"$REPO_ROOT/tests\"          \"$STAGE_APP/tests\"\n\n# strip generated noise (double guard)\nfind \"$STAGE_APP\" -name '__pycache__' -type d -exec rm -rf {} + 2>/dev/null || true\nfind \"$STAGE_APP\" -name '.venv*' -o -name '*.pyc' -delete 2>/dev/null || true\n\n# --- version manifest ----------------------------------------------------------\n{\n  echo \"release_sha=$SHA\"\n  echo \"built_utc=$(date -u +%Y-%m-%dT%H:%M:%SZ)\"\n  echo \"built_by=pdu-manager CI\"\n  echo \"app_modules=$(find \"$STAGE_APP/app\" -maxdepth 1 -name '*.py' | wc -l)\"\n} > \"$STAGE_APP/MANIFEST.txt\"\n\n# --- secret scan gate (fail the build on any hit) ------------------------------\nif grep -rInE '(sk-[A-Za-z0-9_-]{8,}|ghp_[A-Za-z0-9]{20,}|github_pat_[A-Za-z0-9_]{20,}|BEGIN (RSA|OPENSSH|EC) PRIVATE KEY|https?://[^/]*:[^/@]*@)' \"$STAGE_APP\" >/dev/null 2>&1; then\n  echo \"FATAL: secret pattern detected in artifact — refusing to build\" >&2\n  exit 2\nfi\n# b64-credential literal sweep: any _B64=<value> with content (placeholder empty is fine)\nif grep -rInE '^[A-Z_]+_B64=[A-Za-z0-9+/=]{8,}' \"$STAGE_APP\" >/dev/null 2>&1; then\n  echo \"FATAL: encoded credential literal detected — refusing to build\" >&2\n  exit 2\nfi\n\n# --- package --------------------------------------------------------------------\nmkdir -p \"$DIST\"\nTARBALL=\"$DIST/pdu-manager-$SHA.tar.gz\"\ntar -C \"$STAGE\" -czf \"$TARBALL\" \"pdu-manager-$SHA\"\nsha256sum \"$TARBALL\" > \"$DIST/pdu-manager-$SHA.sha256\"\n\necho \"[build-release] artifact: $TARBALL\"\necho \"[build-release] checksum: $(cat \"$DIST/pdu-manager-$SHA.sha256\")\"\necho \"BUILD_OK\"=== CI artifact step ===\n64:      - name: Build release artifact\n67:          ./deploy/build-release.sh \"$(git rev-parse HEAD)\"\n71:      - name: Upload artifact\n72:        uses: actions/upload-artifact@v4", "exit_code": 0, "error": null}

ROLLBACK from stage: $stage\" >&2\n  if [[ -n \"${PREV_RELEASE_SHA:-}\" && -d \"$OPT_DIR/releases/$PREV_RELEASE_SHA\" ]]; then\n    ln -sfn \"$OPT_DIR/releases/$PREV_RELEASE_SHA\" \"$OPT_DIR/current\"\n    # restore the PREVIOUS release's code into the live tree (the switch step\n    # already copied the broken code over /opt/pdu-control; the symlink alone", "exit_code": 0, "error": null}

{"status": "success", "output": "# Authentication helper (Basic over LLDAP)\n# ----------------------------------------------------------------------------\n\ndef _request_id_from():\n    \"\"\"Idempotency-Key header or body request_id, normalized (V3 section 15).\"\"\"\n    rid = request.headers.get(\"Idempotency-Key\", \"\").strip()\n    if rid:\n        return rid[:200]\n    body = request.get_json(silent=True) or {}\n    rid = str(body.get(\"request_id\") or \"\").strip()\n    return rid[:200] if rid else None\n\n\ndef _current_actor_or_response():\n    \"\"\"Returns (Actor, None) on success, (None, Response) on failure.\n\n    Basic-auth only for /api/v1 (V3 section 21: no local-root Basic on the API).\n    \"\"\"\n    auth = request.authorization\n    if not auth or not auth.username or not auth.password:\n        resp = jsonify({\n            \"error\": {\"code\": \"AUTH_REQUIRED\",\n                      \"message\": \"HTTP Basic authentication with LLDAP credentials is required.\"}\n        })\n        resp.status_code = 401\n        resp.headers[\"WWW-Authenticate\"] = 'Basic realm=\"PDU Manager API v1\"'\n        return None, resp\n\n    username = auth.username\n    identity = f\"api:{username}\"\n    kind = \"get\" if request.method in (\"GET\", \"HEAD\") else \"post\"\n\n    # Rate limit BEFORE LDAP bind (protects LLDAP from hammering too)\n    if not _rate_check(identity, kind):\n        return None, _rate_limited_response(kind, identity)\n\n    try:\n        actor = authenticate_lldap(username, auth.password)\n    except LdapUnavailable as exc:\n        resp = jsonify({\n            \"error\": {\"code\": \"AUTH_LDAP_UNAVAILABLE\",\n                      \"message\": f\"LLDAP unreachable: {exc}\"}\n", "tool_calls_made": 1, "duration_seconds": 0.28}

The format is a Markdown batch-selection file, schema version 1:\n139|\n140|- `<!-- PDU_BATCH_ACTION=on|off|reboot -->` — the intended action for the selection;\n141|- `<!-- PDU_BATCH_ITEM=<order>|<ip>|<outlet> -->` — one comment per selected outlet;\n142|- human-readable tables (order/pdu/ip/outlet/label) + full PDU inventory for review.\n143|\n144|Behavior (verified in `app/app.py`):\n145|\n146|- **Export** (`POST /configuration/export`): validates items with action `on` so protected outlets can be saved without implying permission; returns `pdu-master-control-<timestamp>.md` attachment. Contains NO secrets (only asset IDs, IPs, labels).\n147|- **Upload** (`POST /configuration/upload`): accepts `.md/.markdown/.txt` ≤ 1 MB; parses machine-readable comments; validates each target against live config; **rejects OFF/REBOOT selections on protected outlets at upload time**; returns the parsed selection — never executes anything.\n148|- Execution requires an explicit **SEND TO SELECTED** click, which routes through the shared action service (protection re-checked at execution time).\n149|\n150|Live site configuration (outlet labels, protection, PDU inventory) itself lives in `/etc/pdu-control/config.json` — backed up outside the app tree. # AGENTS.md

Instructions for autonomous and semi-autonomous coding agents working in this repository.

## 1. Authority model

Humans set product intent, approve scope, approve significant decisions, and approve merges.

Agents may:

- inspect the repository;
- propose a short plan;
- implement approved requirements;
- create or update automated tests;
- update documentation affected by the change;
- add newly discovered ideas to `FUTURE_WORK.md`;
- draft ADRs and TDRs;
- run non-destructive validation;
- prepare commits and pull requests.

Agents must not:

- silently add product scope;
- promote future work into approved requirements without human approval;
- accept their own newly invented significant architecture decision;
- merge their own work under the current operating model;
- commit directly to `main`/production branches;
- use destructive commands, rotate secrets, alter production data, change permissions, or modify infrastructure without explicit human approval;
- hide failed validation, security concerns, or unresolved trade-offs.

## 2. Read before planning

For every meaningful change, read the relevant parts of:

1. `README.md`
2. `REQUIREMENTS.md`
3. `SPRINT.md`
4. `docs/ARCHITECTURE.md`
5. relevant files in `docs/adr/`
6. relevant files in `docs/tdr/`
7. `FUTURE_WORK.md` when scope or follow-up work is involved

Repository-local instructions and approved requirements are the primary source of truth.

## 3. Requirement discipline

Implementation work should trace to one or more `REQ-###` entries.

Before editing, summarize:

- the requirement IDs being implemented;
- the acceptance criteria;
- the files/components likely to change;
- assumptions or ambiguities;
- the validation that will prove the change works.

If the requested work is not covered by an approved requirement, do one of the following:

- ask for human approval to add/modify the requirement; or
- record the idea in `FUTURE_WORK.md` if it is not part of the current scope.

Do not invent scope merely because it seems useful.

## 4. Planning and execution

Before meaningful edits, provide a short implementation plan. Keep the plan proportional to the change.

During execution:

- work in a dedicated branch/worktree/sandbox;
- keep the diff focused;
- follow the existing architecture and project conventions;
- prefer readable, conventional code over clever code;
- avoid unrelated cleanup;
- preserve backward compatibility unless an approved requirement or ADR says otherwise;
- update affected documentation in the same change.

If the implementation grows materially beyond the approved plan, stop and surface the scope increase.

## 5. Significant decisions and ADRs

Create or update an ADR when a change makes a significant, durable decision about architecture, major dependencies, data/storage, external integrations, security boundaries, deployment model, scalability approach, or another choice that future maintainers are likely to ask "why did we choose this?"

### Human-directed decision

If a human explicitly made the decision, the agent may record it as `Status: Accepted`. The ADR must still be called out in the pull request so the human sees what was recorded.

### Agent-originated decision

If the agent concludes a significant decision is needed, create a draft ADR with `Status: Proposed` and surface it for human approval. Do not treat it as approved until a human accepts it.

Use `docs/adr/TEMPLATE.md`.

## 6. Technical debt and TDRs

Create a TDR when a change intentionally leaves known technical debt such as a shortcut, workaround, temporary design, missing test, manual step, or deferred reliability/security/performance/maintainability improvement.

Use `docs/tdr/TEMPLATE.md` and keep the record concise.

An agent may propose a TDR, but the human reviewer should confirm that the debt is acceptable and that the owner is appropriate.

## 7. Validation

Use the exact validation commands documented by the project once the implementation stack is selected.

Run all checks relevant to the change, such as:

- formatting;
- lint/static analysis;
- type checking where applicable;
- unit tests;
- integration tests;
- end-to-end tests where applicable;
- build/package validation;
- security/secret/dependency checks;
- deployment or readiness checks when relevant.

Report exactly what passed, failed, or was not run. Never imply that a check passed if it was skipped or unavailable.

If expected commands are not documented, surface that as an agent-readiness gap instead of guessing silently.

## 8. Security and data handling

- Do not commit secrets, credentials, private keys, access tokens, or sensitive customer/company data.
- Treat generated shell commands, migrations, dependencies, and configuration changes as untrusted until reviewed.
- Do not weaken authentication, authorization, encryption, logging, isolation, or monitoring merely to make a task easier.
- Surface security-sensitive behavior for explicit human review.

## 9. Git and pull requests

Startup Teams Git policy applies unless a stricter project policy is documented.

- Use a dedicated branch; direct commits to `main`/production branches are prohibited.
- Branch format: `type/ticket-short-description` (for example, `feat/AGR-24-bnpl-checkout`).
- Commit format: `type(scope): short description (#issue-id)`.
- Commit scope is required.
- Every commit must reference the related issue/ticket.
- Keep commits and PRs small enough to review.
- Required validation must pass before merge.
- A human reviewer must approve before merge under the current operating model.

Every PR should state:

- feature / requirement IDs;
- what changed and why;
- files/components changed;
- validation performed and results;
- known risks or limitations;
- documentation changes;
- ADRs/TDRs added or changed;
- future-work items discovered;
- reviewer focus areas.

## 10. Stop and ask for human judgment when

- requirements conflict or are materially ambiguous;
- a new significant ADR decision is required;
- credentials or restricted access are required;
- a destructive action is needed;
- tests fail unexpectedly and the cause is not understood;
- security-sensitive behavior changes;
- production data or infrastructure would be changed;
- the diff grows substantially beyond the planned scope;
- a future-work idea would need to become approved product scope.

## 11. Handoff standard

At the end of a work session or before handing work to another agent/human, provide:

1. requirement IDs addressed;
2. concise summary of changes;
3. files changed;
4. validation commands and results;
5. known risks / unresolved questions;
6. ADR/TDR status;
7. future-work items discovered;
8. recommended next action.

## 12. PDU Manager production safety additions

This application can control real electrical power. Treat production interactions as high-consequence infrastructure operations.

Agents MUST NOT perform real PDU ON/OFF/REBOOT/CYCLE operations as tests unless a human explicitly authorizes the exact outlet/action for that session.

During normal development, source capture, CI, staging, or deployment verification:

- use mocks/fixtures for actuation behavior;
- prefer read-only outlet-state checks when technically enforceable;
- do not reboot VM154 or `MIAM-00133` merely to apply application changes;
- restart only the application service(s) when a service restart is sufficient;
- do not modify production outlet labels, presets, protection/lockout settings, or credentials as an incidental test;
- never place PDU/LDAP/TLS credentials in Git;
- preserve protection of critical power targets;
- treat any change that could remove a safety interlock as security/safety sensitive and stop for human review.

The production endpoint is currently `https://10.0.20.154/` on VM154 hosted by `MIAM-00133`. This location is an approved deployment target, not permission for unrestricted infrastructure changes.

nonzero = failed (gates deploy/rollback).\n# ============================================================================\nset -uo pipefail\n\nBASE_URL=\"${1:-http://127.0.0.1:5000}\"\nMODE=\"${2:-app}\"\nFAILS=0\n\ncheck() {\n  local name=\"$1\" shift_args=(\"$@\")\n  shift\n  if \"$@\" >/dev/null 2>&1; then\n    echo \"PASS  $name\"\n  else\n    echo \"FAIL  $name\"\n    FAILS=$((FAILS+1))\n  fi\n}\n\n# 1. service running\ncheck \"service active\" systemctl is-active pdu-control\n\n# 2. app /health\ncheck \"app /health\" curl -fsS --max-time 10 \"$BASE_URL/health\"\n\n# 3. unauth gates\ncode=$(curl -s -o /dev/null -w '%{http_code}' --max-time 10 \"$BASE_URL/\" || echo 000)\nif [[ \"$code\" == \"302\" || \"$code\" == \"401\" ]]; then\n  echo \"PASS  / unauth gate ($code)\"\nelse\n  echo \"FAIL  / unauth gate ($code)\"\n  FAILS=$((FAILS+1))\nfi\n\ncode=$(curl -s -o /dev/null -w '%{http_code}' --max-time 10 \"$BASE_URL/api/v1/pdus\" || echo 000)\nif [[ \"$code\" == \"401\" ]]; then\n  echo \"PASS  /api/v1/pdus unauth 401\"\nelse\n  echo \"FAIL  /api/v1/pdus unauth ($code — expected 401)\"\n  FAILS=$((FAILS+1))\nfi\n\n# 4. login page renders\ncheck \"login page\" curl -fsS --max-time 10 \"$BASE_URL/login\"\n\nif [[ \"$MODE\" == \"https\" || \"$MODE\" == \"full\" ]]; then\n  HB=\"${BASE_URL/5000/}\"\n  code=$(curl -sk -o /dev/null -w '%{http_code}' --max-time 10 \"https://127.0.0.1/health\" || echo 000)\n  if [[ \"$code\" == \"200\" ]]; then echo \"PASS  https /health via proxy\"; else echo \"FAIL  https /health ($code)\"; FAILS=$((FAILS+1)); fi\n  code=$(curl -sk -o /dev/null -w '%{http_code}' --max-time 10 \"https://127.0.0.1/login\" || echo 000)\n  if [[ \"$code\" == \"200\" ]]; then echo \"PASS  https /login via proxy\"; else echo \"FAIL  https /login ($code)\"; FAILS=$((FAILS+1)); fi\nfi\n", "tool_calls_made": 1, "duration_seconds": 0.03}
